## Document 013: 2026-09-30_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］中国が住宅販売モデルを転換へ_ef0b0461

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-09-30_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］中国が住宅販売モデルを転換へ_ef0b0461.pdf
Report source: Mizuho
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

みずほ経済 EXPRESS

中国が住宅販売モデルを転換へ 
～不動産不況は脱却の兆しなお見えず～

調査部 主任エコノミスト 月岡直樹  
080-1069-6684 naoki.tsukioka@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ 中国政府が新規則を発表。住宅は予約販売から完成後販売にシフト 
中国政府は8月28日、住宅販売制度と住宅ローン管理に関する新規則を発表した。新規の住宅不動産
プロジェクトについて完成後販売の方式を促すほか、未完成物件の予約販売における住宅ローンの実
行時期を物件引き渡し後に変更する。住宅の予約販売制度が不動産ディベロッパーの過度なレバレッ
ジ追求による資金繰り悪化と、それに伴う住宅引き渡し遅延問題を引き起こしたことから、住宅販売
モデルの転換を加速させる。 
住宅都市農村建設部や中国人民銀行、国家金融監督管理総局などの関連省庁が一斉に通達や管理規
定を公布した。住宅販売制度に関する通達では、①予約販売の代金（購入者が支払う頭金と銀行から
振り込まれる住宅ローン）について住宅不動産プロジェクトごとの専用口座を通じた銀行側による資
金管理を強化すること、②新たに不動産用地や建設許可を取得するプロジェクトに対して「完成物件
での販売を優先的に選択」させ、建設許可を取得済みの新規プロジェクトについても完成物件での販
売を奨励すること、③住宅不動産プロジェクトごとに会社を設立し、メインバンクを選定して開発資
金の融資を受けること、などの方針を示した。一方、住宅ローンの新たな管理規定には、予約販売に
おける住宅ローンについて「厳格にプロジェクト竣工届出後に実行しなければならない」と明記した
ほか、これまで最長30年とされてきた住宅ローンの借入期間を40年に変更することを盛り込んだ。 
■ 「保交房」に一定のメド。ディベロッパーの法的整理が進む可能性も 
中国の不動産市場はこれまで、ディベロッパーが物件の竣工を待たずに販売を開始する予約販売方
式が一般的であり、住宅ローンも購入者が予約購入した時点で実行され、返済義務が生じる慣行とな
っていた。予約販売には、購入者にとって不動産価格の値上がり前に物件を確保できるメリットが、
ディベロッパーにとって不動産開発に投じた資金を早期に回収できるメリットがあった。 
しかし、財務レバレッジの追求による収益の最大化を狙うディベロッパーは、回収した予約販売代
金を新たな不動産開発用地の獲得や不動産とは関係のないビジネスへの投資に流用し、事業を急拡大
させた。その実態は自転車操業であり、市場の過熱を懸念した中国政府が2021年にディベロッパーへ
の資金調達規制 （いわゆる 「3つのレッドライン」 ） を正式導入するとたちまち資金繰りに行き詰まり、
社債の債務不履行（デフォルト）が頻発する。ディベロッパーの信用力が低下したところにゼロコロ
2026 年 9 月 30 日

### Page 2

2

ナ政策の強化が追い打ちをかける形で販売が急減し、ディベロッパーの資金繰りが一段と悪化したこ
とで建設工事の進捗が滞り、2022年に入って予約販売済み物件の引き渡し遅延が深刻化する。 
ここに至って予約販売のデメリットが明らかとなる。すなわち、物件引き渡しリスクを負担するの
は購入者であり、たとえ遅延しても住宅ローンの返済を続けなければならないことである。事態を重
く見た中国政府は、「保交楼（予約販売済み未完成物件の引き渡し保証。「保交房」も同じ）」のス
ローガンを掲げ、ディベロッパーの資金繰り支援に乗り出す。2024年1月には、当局が融資適格と判断
した不動産開発プロジェクトをまとめたホワイトリストを作成し、各銀行がこのリストに基づいてデ
ィベロッパーに資金を融通するスキームを構築して、問題の解消を急がせたのである。 
中国政府がこのタイミングで新規則を発表した背景には、この「保交房」に一定のメドが付いたと
判断したことがあるとみられる。事実、国務院の李強首相は2026年3月の全人代における政府活動報告
で「『保交房』の任務は全面的に完成した」と宣言している。また、見逃せない動きとして、広東省
広州市の中級人民法院（地方裁判所）が8月21日、実質的に経営破綻している某ディベロッパーについ
て、債権者による清算の申し立てを受理したことが挙げられる。 
デフォルトを起こした不動産ディベロッパーの多くはこれまで、倒産によって債務の返済や予約販
売物件の引き渡し、建設業者への代金支払いなどの義務・責任を放棄することが許されず、債権者か
ら債務の減免や返済猶予の合意を得つつゾンビ化した状態で経営を続けてきた。ディベロッパーの破
綻が相次げば、不良債権問題の深刻化によって金融システムが動揺するリスクを招くだけでなく、予
約販売物件の引き渡しが困難となって社会問題に発展したり、下請け業者の連鎖倒産で景気が急激に
冷え込む可能性もあったからである。 
今回の動きは、清算の申し立てを裁判所が「受理」しただけではあり、実際に清算が進むのかはな
お予断を許さないが、中国政府がディベロッパーの法的整理を前に進める意思を示したとみることも
できる。今後、清算手続きが順調に進むのか、他のディベロッパーについても同様の動きが広がるの
かを注意深く見守る必要があろう。 
■ 住宅販売モデル転換の社会的意義は大きいが、不動産市況への影響は軽微 
では、住宅販売モデルの転換は不動産市場にどのような影響を与えるであろうか。その前にまず、
不動産市場の現状を整理しておきたい。 
資金調達規制の強化をきっかけとした不動産不況は6年目に突入した。中国の不動産市場規模（販売
金額）は2021～25年にかけて半分に縮小し、販売面積や開発投資額は4割、新規着工面積に至っては7
割も減少した（図表1）。2026年も不況脱却の兆しは見えず、1～8月の販売面積は前年比▲12.1％、開
発投資額は同▲19.9％と低迷が続いている。開発投資の先行指標とされる不動産開発向け銀行貸出も
同▲33.3％となっており、ディベロッパーにとって資金繰りが厳しい状況は変わっていない。 
販売が大きく落ち込んだことで、市場には過剰な在庫が積み上がっている。みずほ総合研究所調査
部の試算によれば、建設中の物件を含む中国の住宅在庫（商用およびオフィス用を除く）は2025年末
時点で40.8億平米に達する（図表2）。2025年の住宅販売面積が7.3億平米であったことから、新規着工
がゼロになると仮定しても在庫をすべて消化するのに5年以上かかる計算である。中国政府は2024年5
月、地方政府による住宅在庫の買い取り策を発表し、同年10月には地方政府が専項債（特別債）の発
行で得た資金を買い取りに使うことも容認したが、在庫の消化は遅々として進んでいない。

### Page 3

3

住宅価格（主要70都市平均）は2021年7月のピーク時から新築で▲14％、中古で▲23％下落し、全体
として下落傾向に歯止めがかかっていない。ただ、図表3のとおり、底堅い需要のある大都市（一級都
市：北京、上海、広州、深圳）と膨大な在庫を抱える地方都市（二級都市：各省の省都。三級都市：
省都以下の主要都市）で二極化が顕著になっている。特に上海は堅調で、その新築住宅価格は2021年
7月から＋24％も上昇している。とはいえ、その堅調さは資産としての不動産保有の魅力を有する一部
の都市に限ったものであり、市況の回復が地方都市にまで広く波及する様子はうかがえない。 
住宅引き渡し遅延問題の発生以降、引き渡しリスクのない完成物件の購入が増加しつつあるが、予
約販売は2025年時点でも依然として住宅販売面積全体の6割超を占めている（図表4）。住宅販売モデ
ルの転換、すなわち予約販売から完成後販売への移行と予約販売における住宅ローン実行の後ろ倒し
は、住宅購入者の物件引き渡しリスクを大幅に軽減することになり、消費者保護の観点からもその社
会的意義は非常に大きいといえる。しかし、これに伴ってディベロッパーの資金繰りはより厳しいも
のとなる。ディベロッパーの資金調達に占める販売代金（頭金などの前受金と住宅ローン）の比率は
4割を超えているからである（図表5）。このため、短期的には新規の開発（土地の取得や新規着工）

が減少する可能性が高い。ただ、新規則は完成
後販売を全国一律に「強制」するのではなく、
あくまで「優先的に選択」させるとしているこ
とから、地方によってはディベロッパーの資金
繰りにも配慮した柔軟な対応がとられる可能
性もある。 
新規制が不動産市況に与える影響は、住宅ロ
ーンの最長借入期間の延長が新たな需要を掘
り起こすとしても、軽微であろう。不動産不況
を脱するためには、過剰在庫の消化とディベロ
ッパーの法的整理、不良債権の処理を着実に進
め、市場を健全な状態に戻すことが不可欠とい
えるが、それにはまだ相当の時間を要するもの
とみられる。

營收走勢圖

研究員/陳晏平 TINA.YP.CHEN@uni-psg.com

1H26 主要認列案源為中和 K Tower Y09 商辦案、中和心禾匯住宅
案、台北信義豪宅案持續去化與遞延交屋，1H26 累積營收為 132 億
元 ， 成長31%YOY， 且在整體毛利率較2025 年同期些微提升下 ，1H26
累積稅後淨利為 11.1 億元，成長 61%YOY，EPS 為 1.87 元。展望
2H26，兩個台中新完工建案(合計總銷金額 83 億元)遞延至 4Q26 才
正式推出銷售，且台中市地區為 2026 年以來銷售速度較慢的區域，
預期此兩個台中建案在 2026 年認列案量較少，主要將貢獻在 2027
年。所幸，近幾個月財富效果開始顯現，進而有提升居住品質、資
產配置需求，台北市信義豪宅案 1H26 已認列逾 10 億元，銷售優於
預期 。 因此 ， 研究部預估2026 年合併營收為 268 億元 ，成長 17%YOY，
稅後淨利 20 億元，成長 27%YOY，EPS 為 3.24 元，獲利將自谷底回
升；並預估 2027 年合併營收為 301 億元，成長 12%YOY，稅後淨利
為 24 億元，成長 21%YOY，EPS 為 3.92 元，營運將呈現穩增。投資
建議方面，雖目前房市相關政策已不再緊縮，並朝向逐步鬆綁，加
上高科技產業景氣成長強勁、股市上漲帶來財富效果，使得房市氣
氛將逐漸走出谷底，但因研究部預期冠德 2026~2027 年獲利皆將落
在 3~4 元之譜，未明顯跳增，近兩年現金殖利率亦約當 4~5%，未明
顯高於同業，故維持給予0.5xNAV，目標價40 元，潛在上漲空間有
限，重申中立建議。

重點摘要： 
1. 1H26認列案源主要為帳上成屋持續去化，累積稅後 EPS為1.87
元： 
受惠:(1) 中和K Tower Y09( 原中和秀朗橋捷運聯開商辦案，
總銷金額31億元，銷售率30%)新完工建案開始交屋；(2) 中和
心禾匯(原中和LG08捷運聯開住宅案，總銷金額16億元，銷售
率80%)、 台北市冠德信義豪宅案持續去化與遞延交屋入帳 ；(3)
轉投資根基營造合併營收貢獻大增75%QOQ、52%YOY至61億元，
進而帶動2Q26單季合併 營收明顯成長72%QOQ、57%YOY至84億
元。另在營造子公司有個案結算以及高毛利率冠德信義豪宅案
認列較多下 ， 毛利率亦有所提升，2Q26單季毛利率上揚至28%，
季增4個百分點、年增5個百分點，再扣除費用、所得稅負、根
基少數股權影響，2Q26單季稅後淨利為8億元(歸屬母公司)，
跳增160%QOQ、224%YOY，單季EPS為1.35元。

合計1H26累積營收為132億元，成長31%YOY，且在整體毛利率
較2025年同期些微提升下，1H26累積稅後淨利為11.1億元，成
長61%YOY，EPS為1.87元。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

2. 兩個台中新完工建案遞延至4Q26才正式推出銷售， 預估2026年
稅後EPS將自谷底回升至3.24元： 
展望2H26，除帳上成屋案持續去化外，冠德 規劃10~11月將陸
續推出台中裕毛屋住宅案(總銷金額67億元)、 台中G5捷運聯開
商辦案(總銷金額16億元)、台北市萬華直興住宅 案(總銷金額
36億元)，合計新推出案量共119億元。其中，台中裕毛屋住宅
案已於2026年6月取得使照，並有先行潛銷，預計2026年10月
正式推出銷售 ；台中G5捷運聯開商辦案亦已取得使照，計畫
2026年11月開始銷售 ，此兩案皆 將視銷售成績在2H26交屋入
帳。

考量:(1) 台中為2026年以來房屋銷售較慢的區域，且台中裕
毛屋住宅案、台中G5捷運聯開商辦案皆延至4Q26才開始銷售，
預期2026年認列比例將較原先預期少，推估將分別認列 20%、
10%案量；(2) 中和心禾匯、中和K Tower Y09可於2026年順利
銷售並完成交屋25%、1/3案量；(3) 財富效果顯現，進而有提
升居住品質、資產配置需求，預期台北市冠德信義豪宅案可去
化20億元金額，較2025年認列金額倍增。再加計營造、百貨事
業營收貢獻 ， 預估2026年合併營收為268億元 ，成長17%YOY，稅
後淨利20億元，成長27%YOY，EPS為3.24元，將自谷底回升。

3. 2027年將有一台北市住宅案完工 ，加計帳上成屋去化， 預估
2027年稅後EPS將穩增至3.92元： 
展望2027年，除持續銷售前述個案外，預期4Q27還將新推出南
港台電公辦都更住宅案，該案總銷金額達130億元，將視銷售
情況於2028年完工之時認列。而在建案認列方面，預期將有總
銷金額36億元的台北市萬華直興住宅案於3Q27完工 ， 研究部採
保守原則，推估此案在2027年將可順利去化並認列30%案量，
加計:(1)中和心禾匯、中和K Tower Y09、台中裕毛屋住宅案、
台中G5捷運聯開商辦案 ， 推估將分別去化 、 認列15%、1/3、60%、
60%案量 ；(2)台北市冠德信義豪宅案遞延交屋與持續去化20億
元；(3) 營造 、 百貨事業營收貢獻， 預估2027年合併營收為301
億元，成長12%YOY，稅後淨利為24億元，成長21%YOY，EPS為
3.92元，營運將呈現穩增。

4. 帳上土地庫存充足，估計每股資產價值為77元，目前股價評價
約0.5xNAV： 
在扣除2027年完工建案後，帳上土地庫存仍有2.2萬坪，換算
總銷金額為801億元 ， 未來推案無虞 。 就區域分布上 ， 以台北市
佔比最高，高達62%，其次為高雄市、台中市，佔比各為15%、

案、南港台電公辦都更住宅案、台北市行二行三公辦都更案、

5. 預期2027年現金殖利率將在4.4~4.9%區間水準： 
股利政策方面，為保留資本，以提高自有資金比例、降低營運
槓桿，近幾年在正常獲利情況下，現金股利配發率皆落在
35~41%區間水準 。 而因2025年獲利衰退較多 ，2026年配發1.616
元現金股利 ， 且無股票股利 ， 現金股利配發率提升至62%， 已於
2026/8/10除息，除息前現金殖利率4.7%。由於2026年獲利將
自谷底回升，研究部預期2027年現金股利將落在1.6~1.8元區
間水準，換算現金殖利率4.4~4.9%。

## Document 107: 44亿港元资产被限制！茶百道遭香港证监会停牌：IPO涉嫌人为操纵，募资款或暗中回流至承配人

Source PDF: all report pdf/44亿港元资产被限制！茶百道遭香港证监会停牌：IPO涉嫌人为操纵，募资款或暗中回流至承配人.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-13
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

⾹港证监会怀疑茶百道 2024 年 IPO 涉嫌⼈为操纵股份需求，已下令其⾃ 10 ⽉ 13 ⽇上午 9 时
起停牌，并对相关可疑集团发出资产限制通知，涉及⾦额分别达 43.79 亿港元和 2.85 亿港
元。茶百道上市时公开发售认购仅 0.5 倍，股价较发⾏价已跌近 77% 。证监会调查仍在进⾏
中，相关指控尚待进⼀步查证。
摘要：
茶百道 IPO 疑遭⼈为操纵，被⾹港证监会下令停牌。
10 ⽉ 13 ⽇，据⾹港证监会官⽹，⾹港证券及期货事务监察委员会（以下简称 “ ⾹港证监会 ” ）已对
两个集团采取⾏动，因它们涉嫌参与欺诈计划，意图就⾸次公开招股的股份需求营造虚假或⾮
真实的表象。⾹港证监会已根据相关规定，⾃今天上午 9 时正起暂停四川百茶百道实业股份有限
公司的股份交易。
⾹港证监会表⽰，茶百道的⾸次公开招股可能曾被⼈为操纵，意图就茶百道的股份需求营造⾮
真实的表象。在证监会继续进⾏调查期间，暂停茶百道的股份交易，对为该股维持⼀个有秩序
和公平的市场及维护投资⼤众的利益是有需要或合宜的。

⾹港证监会还披露了具体细节。就第⼀个可疑集团⽽⾔，⾹港证监会已向六家经纪⾏发出限制
通知书，禁⽌它们处置或处理孙然及⼋家相关公司名下客⼾账⼾内的资产，以 43.79 亿港元为限
（⾹港证监会历来同类案件中的最⾼限制⾦额）。第⼀个可疑集团涉嫌参与有关拨康视云制药有
限公司（拨康视云）及茶百道的⾸次公开招股的欺诈计划。
就第⼆个可疑集团⽽⾔，⾹港证监会已向 29 家经纪⾏发出限制通知书，禁⽌它们处理或处置刘
浩然等三个实体名下客⼾账⼾内的资产，以 2.85 亿港元为限。这可疑集团涉嫌参与有关天聚地合
（苏州）科技股份有限公司（天聚地合）的⾸次公开招股的欺诈计划。
⾹港证监会指出，有关可疑集团涉嫌参与欺诈计划，当中部分⾸次公开招股所得款项可能已根
据上市公司与该等集团之间未有披露的协议，被转离有关公司，作所谓的⾦融产品投资之⽤。
该等协议可能促使⾸次公开招股所得款项暗中回流⾄主要⾸次公开招股承配⼈或其关连⼈⼠。
收到限制通知书的经纪⾏并⾮证监会调查的对象。有关限制通知书并不影响该等经纪⾏的运作

或它们的其他客⼾。
⾹港证监会表⽰，就维护投资⼤众的利益或公众利益⽽⾔，发出限制通知书以保存有关资产是
可取的做法。所涉各⽅⽇后可能会被要求通过复还程序，向受影响的投资者作出赔偿。
需要指出的是，⾹港证监会提到了三家上市公司，除茶百道之外，拨康视云已于 9 ⽉ 10 ⽇停牌，
天聚地合则从 2025 年 4 ⽉ 1 ⽇暂停交易。
2008 年，第⼀家茶百道在成都诞⽣。 2017 年 2 ⽉，茶百道餐饮成⽴。茶百道于 2024 年 4 ⽉ 23 ⽇
在港股上市，成为当时登陆港交所的 “ 加盟制茶饮第⼀股 ” ，上市⾸⽇破发，股价⼤跌逾 26% ，市
值蒸发近 70 亿港元⾄ 189 亿港元。截⾄ 10 ⽉ 12 ⽇，茶百道报收 4.035 港元，较 17.5 港元发⾏价下
跌近 77% ，今年以来股价已跌超 36% ，市值为 60 亿港元。
此次⾹港证监会怀疑，茶百道 2024 年的⾸次公开招股可能曾被⼈为操纵，意图就其股份需求营
造⾮真实的表象。值得注意的是，茶百道当年的公开认购数据并未显⽰出旺盛的市场需求。

根据茶百道 2024 年 4 ⽉ 15 ⽇在港交所披露的招股章程，公司计划全球发售约 1.48 亿股 H 股，其中
⾹港公开发售 1477.64 万股，占⽐约 10% ；国际发售约 1.33 亿股，占⽐约 90% ，发售价为每股
17.50 港元。
根据公司 2024 年 4 ⽉ 22 ⽇披露的《分配结果公告》，⾹港公开发售共收到 4593 份有效申请，认购
⽔平为 0.5 倍；国际发售认购⽔平为 1.11 倍，共有 122 名承配⼈。由于⾹港公开发售认购不⾜，
744.08 万股被重新分配⾄国际发售，最终⾹港公开发售股份数⽬调整为 733.56 万股，国际发售
股份数⽬增⾄约 1.40 亿股。扣除上市费⽤后，茶百道此次 IPO 净募资额约为 24.63 亿港元。

经营业绩来看，茶百道今年上半年实现营收 26.55 亿元，同⽐增 6.2% ；期内净利润为 3.45 亿元，
同⽐增 3.5% 。去年实现营收 53.95 亿元，同⽐增 9.7% ；期内净利润为 8.05 亿元，同⽐增超七
成。

材料 *-KY (4763 TT)  2Q26 因山東廠停工歲修，毛利率降至  40.9% ， 3Q26 已回升至近  
50%；扣 除 匯 回 美 元 3 億 資 本 之 一 次 性 稅 款 約 新 台 幣 9.6 億元後 ，稅後 EPS 0.72 元 。
2027 年併入  Cerdia 全 球 產 能 後，年 產 能 達 絲 束 16.40 萬噸與醋片  19.50 萬噸 。全
球 絲 束 市 場 由 五 大 業 者 濃 縮 為 四 大 業 者 ， 公 司 採 取 控 制 接 單 策 略 ， 預計  10 ~11 /2026

A51755@cim.capital.com.tw

材料 *-KY 法 說 會 展 望 正 面， 4Q26 將 調 整 2027 年 合 約 價 格：  
材 料 *-KY 於  09/ 21 /2026 宣布以約美元  3.68 億 收 購 全 球 醋 酸 纖 維 大 廠  
Cerdia 100% 股 權 。 交 易 架 構 由 兩 部 分 構 成， 材料 -KY 以 自 有 資 金 支 付 現
金 對 價 約 美 元 2.14 億 ， 佔 比 約  58% ； 其 餘 發 行 新 股 109,295 仟股 ， 佔比
約  42% ， 交 易 完 成 後 賣 方 黑 石 集 團 實 體 將 持 有 材 料 -KY 增 資 後 約  9.95% 
股 權 ， 集 團 股 本 由 9.89 億 股 增 至 10.98 億股。   
被 收 購 方  Cerdia 2025 年 營 收 為 美 元  7.31 億 ， 調 整 後  EBITDA 為 美 元  
2.67 億， EBITDA 利潤率為  36.5% 。 截 至  06/2026 之近  12 個 月 ， 雙 方
合 計 營 收 約 達 10 億 美 元 ， 合 併 EBITDA 達 4 億 美 元 ， 合 併 EBITDA 利
潤率為  39% 。 此 交 易 使 材 料-KY 由 原 中 國 山 東 單 一 生 產 基 地 ， 擴 增 至 德 國
弗萊堡、美國金斯波特、巴西聖安德烈及俄羅斯謝爾普霍夫等全球五大基
地 。 合 併 後 全 球 醋 酸 纖 維 絲 束 設 計 產 能 達 16.40 萬噸 /年 ， 醋 酸 纖 維 素 醋 片
設 計 產 能 達 19.50 萬噸 /年。   
財 務 結 構 改 善 為 本 次 收 購 的 核 心 獲 利 驅 動 力 。Cerdia 目 前 帳 上 存 續 美 元 9 
億 私 債，到 期 日 為 2031 年，票 面 利 率 約 9%，年 利 息 支 出 達 美 元 0.81 億。
管 理 層 規 劃 於  2027 年 交 割 並 表 後 進 行 債 務 再 融 資 ， 透 過 台 系 與 歐 系 銀 行
將 利 率 降 至 4.5% –6% ， 預 估 每 年 節 省 約 美 元 0.41 億 利 息 支 出 ， 扣 稅 後 將
直 接 挹 注 合 併 淨 利 。 
材料 *-KY 2Q26 毛 利 率 降 至 40.9% ，主 因 山 東 各 廠 進 行 3 週至  1 個 月 歲
修 ， 停 工 固 定 費 用 全 數 列 入 當 期 銷 貨 成 本 ，3Q26 毛 利 率 已 回 升 至 近 五 成 ；
3Q26 另 認 列 自 山 東 匯 回 美 元 3 億 資 本 之 10% 一 次 性 所 得 稅， 約 為 0.30 
億美元 (約 新 台 幣 9.6 億元 )， 不 影 響 本 業 長 期 獲 利 。  
產業與產品布局上，全球絲束市場由五大業者濃縮為四大業者。 公司 採取
調控供給策略，將山東部分產能轉向長絲、短絲及不織布，維持絲束市場
供給緊縮以支撐產品價格。收購宣布後引發下游避險訂單，公司採取控制
接單策略，將談判遞延至  10/2026 至  11/2026 進行  2027 年 合 約 調 價 。
產品分工方面，材料 -KY 負 責 長 絲 、 短 絲 及 塑 膠 級 醋 片 ， 切 入 紡 織 、 眼 鏡
框與  AI 穿戴材料； Cerdia 則主打  85 種濾嘴規格及  100% 生物可降解
濾 嘴。材 料 *-KY 具 備 眼 鏡 膠 框 熱 解 回 收 專 利，正 與 東 莞 大 型 眼 鏡 廠 合 作 處
理每年約  1.65 萬 噸 廢 料 ， 預 計 12/2026 正 式 公 告 。

產業速報

3 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】 
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy)  5%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等 
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整 
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】 
  投 資 評 等 係 「 首 次 」 給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時 ， 其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較3 個
月目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為 四 個等級，定義如
上 。 爾 後 的 投 資 評 等係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」及「 區 間 操 作 」均有 upside 目 標 價 。差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」 或「區間操作」 之投資評
等，就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基
本 面 及 股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。 
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ： 
1.  達 目 標 價 。 
2.  雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ，
研 判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。 
3.  推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 ， 亦 將 出 具 降 評 報 告 。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。
我們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來
預測之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投
資 目 的 或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的 ，
既非對投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提
供詢價服務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值
及收益，可能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投
資 來 得 少 的 情 形 。

休閒車新訂單與電子新產品挹注成長 
 2H26 持續受惠美國 Honda 新訂單放量出貨 ， 以及越南電動機車客戶
產能供不應求的外包訂單需求 ， 營收可望逐季墊高 。 推估下半年平均
每月營收 8~8.5 億元。 
 休閒車產品 2026 年在客戶 Polaris 調整告一段落以及北美 Honda 與
哈雷新訂單開始挹注下，預期營收貢獻將成長 50%以上，2027 年北
美 Honda 還有其他車型將量產，推估可再成長 20-30%。 
 相關機櫃與散熱產品將於 2Q26 陸續少量出貨 ，2027 年將放量出貨 。
預估電子產品營收佔比年底可增至 3-5%。因應電子與大型自動化產
線需求越南百善二廠二期擴建，預計 3Q27 末完工，推估 2027 年營
收比重可達近雙位數。 
 展望 2026 年，受惠越南市場受惠發展電動二輪政策，以及受惠北美
Honda 與哈雷新訂單挹注。預期 2026 年 EPS 17.25 元。2027 年休
閒車新訂單挹注以及電子產品隨著產能開出放量 ， 預期EPS 21.3 元。 
 維持買進 ：公司受惠北美 HONDA 新訂單與電動機車客戶轉單挹
注，今明兩年營運轉好。此外，受惠供應鏈由中國轉移至越南，公司
越南廠具在地優勢取得電子新訂單 ， 隨著越南百善二廠廠能開出 ， 將
挹注未來成長動能。維持買進建議，目標價 234 元。

元富證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市敦化南路二段 97 號 19 樓 (02)2325-3299  (115)金管投顧新字第 016 號         Page 2 of 5 
個股報告

北美新訂單挹注，營運逐季墊高

越南電動二輪政策需求， 彌補燃
油機車缺口

公司 2Q26 營收 24.1 億元 ，+16.9%QoQ，+53.1%YoY， 主要為休閒車輛訂單回溫 ，
加上越南機車業務拉貨回升。在稼動率提升與產品結構轉好下，毛利率23.8%較上
季好轉，單季 EPS 4.42 元。

2H26 持續受惠美國 Honda 新訂單放量出貨 ， 以及越南電動機車客戶產能供不應求
的外包訂單需求 ， 營收可望逐季墊高 。 推估下半年平均每月營收8~8.5 億元 ，3Q26
營收 24.34 億元，+1%QoQ，+58.8%YoY，EPS 4.72 元。4Q26 為越南機車產業
旺季，預計營收 25.07 億元，+3%QoQ，+38.9%YoY，EPS 4.53 元。

1H26 主要產品營收佔比依序為機車 44.8%、 休閒車43.9%、 健康8.6%、 汽車2.6%。

機車產品方面，越南傳統機車市場為主，主要客戶為 YAMAHA 與 Honda，2025
年受到 HONDA 下修生產計劃影響，營收貢獻較前一年下滑 14%。2026 年越南河
內與胡志明市將禁止傳統燃油二輪，轉為發展電動二輪市場，雖影響傳統燃油機車
市場，但受惠政策，新的電動二輪換車需求彌補了油車下滑的缺口。  
目前客戶 Vinfast 產能吃緊釋出訂單 ，1H26 機車產品營收貢獻成長 22%， 預期2026
年機車營收可成長 16-17%。2027 年越南 Honda 將有電動二輪產品推出，有利維
持機車產品成長。

北美 Honda 與哈雷為休閒車產品
主要成長動能

汽車與健康事業轉佳

休閒車：主要客戶為 Polaris、Harley 與北美 Honda，受惠客戶為降低成本與避開
當地關稅的策略，將產線移至越南，以及美國當地非紅供應鏈政策，相關供應鏈由
中國轉向越南。而 2025 年在川普總統推動美國製造下，公司調整產線並於美國南
卡羅來納州設廠因應。2025 年受到通膨影響北美休閒車市場需求趨緩，主要客戶
Polaris 調整庫存，目前庫存水位已將至低位，自 1Q26 客戶陸續補庫存。

新訂單方面，北美 Honda ATV 零件，目前接單規劃至 2028 年都有新專案出貨。
由於 Honda ATV 在北美規模與 Polaris 相當，為未來成長的主要客戶與動能，新
訂單於 2Q26 量產，預估營收貢獻將從 2025 年的 4.9 億元大幅增至 2026 年的 
12~13 億元。此外，公司哈雷訂單於 1Q26 開始出貨，推估 2026 年可貢獻 9-10
億元，目前仍有新項目在開發，將挹注未來營運動能。

休閒車產品 2026 年在客戶 Polaris 調整告一段落以及北美 Honda 與哈雷新訂單開
始挹注下，預期營收貢獻將成長 50%以上。2027 年除了北美 Honda SXS 與 ATV
訂單外，還有其他車型將量產，加上哈雷，與歐洲、日本的新訂單，推估休閒車營
收貢獻可再成長 20-30%。

汽車相關產品取得 Toyota、Honda 與 Vinfast 電動車訂單，加上其他車廠未來也
規劃將組裝線設於越南， 公司看好未來越南與東協車用市場成長， 透過與相關供應
鏈的台商合資取得供應商資格，未來將可從業外認列轉投資收益。 2025 年受到越
南需求不如預期影響 ， 營收貢獻僅成長6%，2026 年越南車用市場回穩 ， 加上Vinfast

Unit approvals drive the headline lower
30 September 2026
Key points
• Total dwelling approvals fell 6.1%mth in
August, with the annual pace easing to
10.3%yr. The headline decline was driven
by a sharp fall in private unit approvals.

• Private detached dwelling approvals
rose 3.7%mth, reaching their highest
level since August 2021. In contrast,
private unit approvals fell 21.2%mth.
• Queensland recorded the weakest
result, while NSW also posted
a sizeable decline. By contrast,
WA, Victoria and South Australia
recorded gains over the month.
• The value of residential building
approvals fell 0.6%mth. Volatility
in the data centre dominated ‘other
commercial buildings’ segment
contributed to the total value of

building approvals falling 21.3%mth.
• While detached dwelling approvals
continue to show resilience, recent
RBA cash rate increases continue
to present risks to the outlook.

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Total dwelling approvals fell 6.1%mth in August, with the
annual pace easing slightly to 10.3%yr. The result was broadly
in line with Westpac’s expectation for a 5.0%mth decline,
although notably weaker than the market expectation for a
1.0%mth fall. July’s decline in private detached dwellings

proved short lived, with approvals rebounding in August.
Instead, weakness in the headline result was driven by a
sizeable decline in private unit approvals.
Note that most figures in the charts and table are presented on
a rolling-three month basis to smooth out monthly volatility.
Private detached dwelling approvals rose 3.7%mth in August,
following a large 2.5ppt upward revision to July’s 1.9%mth
decline. The increase was the strongest in more than a year,

with private detached dwelling approvals reaching their highest
level since August 2021. With the annual pace strengthening
to 18.4%yr, detached dwelling approvals continue to appear
resilient despite recent interest rate increases. Some of this
resilience may partly reflect the implementation of changes to
SMSF property borrowing arrangements on 10 August.
Private unit approvals drove the decline in the headline result,
falling 21.2%mth in August. Following a 1.2ppt upward

revision to July’s 0.8%mth increase, the annual pace returned to
contractionary territory at –2.1%yr. Weakness was broad-based
across segments, with both high-rise and low-mid-rise
approvals declining in the month.
Queensland recorded the weakest result, with private approvals
falling 22.6%mth, driven primarily by weakness in unit approvals,
while private approvals in NSW also declined, falling 17.1%mth.
By contrast, WA (+0.2%mth), Victoria (+8.4%mth) and South

Australia (+32.0%mth) all recorded gains over the month.
In value terms, residential building approvals fell 0.6%mth,
driven by a 0.5%mth decline in new residential approvals
and a 2.1%mth fall in renovation activity. Non-residential
approvals declined sharply, down 44.8%mth. Volatility
continues to be driven by the data centre-dominated ‘other
commercial’ segment, which fell 91.1%mth on a non-seasonally
adjusted basis. Excluding this segment, in non-seasonally

adjusted terms, non-residential approvals fell a more subdued
13.9%mth (from -37.9%mth). The total value of building
approvals declined 21.3%mth in August.
August’s result suggests dwelling approvals continue to
moderate following the strength recorded earlier in the year.
However, the underlying picture remains more resilient, with
the headline decline driven by volatility in unit approvals
while detached dwellings strengthened. NSW and Victoria,

traditionally among the more interest rate sensitive housing
markets, recorded the weakest detached dwelling outcomes,
potentially signalling broader rate effects ahead. For a
comprehensive assessment of housing market conditions, see
our latest Housing Pulse (see here).
Detached approvals point to some resilience

## Document 203: Chartpack _ Advanced economies_ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-10-06)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Chartpack _ Advanced economies_ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-10-06).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

By Advanced Economies Team
Advanced Economies:
Resilience to shock after shock
October 2026

### Page 2

• The global economy is in the midst of a growth cycle. As a result, GDP growth is more resilient to shocks than initially
anticipated.
• The figures for the second quarter bear this out, despite the sharp rise in oil prices, whose negative impact on growth is offset
by a series of reflationary forces.
• The driving force behind the current growth cycle is clear: artificial intelligence (AI).
• The world’s major economies are currently engaged in a broader push to expand their capital goods investment.

• While European and US households are experiencing more or less the same energy price shock, inflation and household
consumption remain higher in the US.
• Higher and above-target inflation calls for monetary tightening.
• Inflation is exceeding the target set by three of the four central banks examined in this publication (the US, the UK, the
Eurozone). Inflation is currently expected to peak in Q4 2026 or Q1 2027, but there is a significant risk that energy inflation will

spread further to other components of inflation in the future.
• Inflation can also be a sign of (too much) economic strength (in the US).
• Monetary policies also have to offset the inflationary effects of fiscal policy (particularly in Japan).
• The outbreak of the conflict in Iran acted as a catalyst: the ensuing rise in energy prices contributes to the recent increase in
long-term rates (which had been trending downwards at the start of the year) to levels close to those of 2002.

• The other issue is the high level of fiscal deficits (too high for a period of growth), reflecting different fiscal trajectories. In the
US, the UK, France and Germany the public debt-to-GDP ratio is increasing, while it has stabilized in Italy and decreased in
Spain and Japan.
• The rise in long-term interest rates will weigh on public finances. The fiscal arithmetic is simpler today than it will be in 2030.
By 2030, the effective rate will have risen and caught up with GDP growth.

For more information, please refer to our publication:
EcoPerspectives – Advanced Economies | 3rd quarter of 2026, as of September 24, 2026– Economic Research– BNP Paribas
Advanced economies: Resilience to shock after shock

## Document 209: Cliff Notes_ searching for signal (2026-10-13)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ searching for signal (2026-10-13).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: searching for signal
By Sian Fenner, Ryan Wells & Illiana Jain
13:33 October 02 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia, this week’s main event was the RBA Monetary Policy Board’s (MPB) decision to raise the
cash rate by 25bps to 4.60%. This outcome was universally expected given Governor Bullock had
already communicated that upside risks to inflation had materialised – the conditionality established by

the MPB back in August for considering another rate hike.

### Page 2

Governor Bullock made explicit note of three upside risks. The Middle East conflict was emphasised as
the risk that had “materialised”, given the re-escalation and renewed surge in crude oil and refined
product prices over recent months. The RBA’s liaison warns this could lead to further pass-through of
costs and more persistent inflation pressures. On top of this, domestic capacity pressures and the data
centre/AI investment boom were also noted as upside risks that the MPB remain alert to.

In a video update mid-week, Chief Economist Luci Ellis explains why the bar for a follow-up rate hike in
November is low. We now expect a 25bp increase in November, provided there is no sudden resolution
of the Middle East conflict beforehand, or some other event that significantly lowers the outlook for
energy-related costs.
Subsequently, the August CPI revealed that underlying (trimmed mean) inflation rose 0.2% (3.4%yr),
which was slightly below the market’s expectation but spot on Westpac’s forecast. As usual, the

monthly detail was mixed, but the key drivers of underlying inflation remain centred on housing-related
components, particularly rents and new dwelling purchases. Headline inflation remained volatile, with
the unwind of the fuel excise cut causing another spike in fuel prices. We have maintained our forecast
for a 0.9% lift in trimmed mean inflation in Q3, and with upside risks building for Q4, a follow-up rate
hike in November appears most likely.

Regarding the current growth pulse, household spending surprised with a flat reading in August.
Services spend fell back following recent strength that was driven by major sporting events, but this
was offset by an increase in goods spend, particularly in fuel and electric vehicles. Together with our
latest card data for early September, the consumer spending pulse is shading a touch softer. However,
another solid outcome for business investment could once again be on the cards for Q3. August’s

goods trade data showcased another significant spike in data centre-related equipment imports, up
$4.3bn, similar in scale to Q1. While clearly import-driven, our modelling suggests that the local
economic impact of such investment is likely to be around half of the total investment.
Before moving offshore, a final note on housing. Cotality’s latest update revealed that dwelling prices
fell another 1.1% nationally in September. While Sydney and Melbourne have led the correction to

date, the fact that the other capitals are also reporting price declines clearly indicates that the
correction is deepening and broadening, as higher interest rates collide with the tax policy changes
announced in May’s budget. Sentiment effects indeed remain at play, as evinced by the continued pull-
back in investor credit growth. Westpac anticipates a peak-to-trough decline of around 7%, with tight
on-market supply limiting the extent of the correction. For more, see our latest Housing Pulse.

Offshore, there was plenty of data to dissect.
In the US, inflation eased in August despite ongoing strength in household demand and higher energy
prices. Headline PCE inflation rose 0.3%mth and 3.4%yr, while core PCE increased 0.2%mth and
3.0%yr. Encouragingly for the FOMC, the share of PCE components rising by more than 3% over the
past year, a measure frequently cited by Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh, declined to 45% from 49%,
suggesting some easing in broad-based inflation pressures. At the same time, household spending

remained robust, increasing 0.9%mth, equivalent to around 0.6%mth in real terms. That said, personal
income growth was soft, rising 0.2% in the month, signalling households opted to draw down savings.

### Page 3

The ISM manufacturing survey was stronger than the headline suggested. While the index edged down
0.1pt to 54.5, manufacturing has now expanded for nine consecutive months. There were some signs
of capacity constraints emerging, with production softened in the month but backlogs up 4.6pts to 56.4,
suggesting that firms struggled to keep pace with demand. New orders rose 1.6pts to 55.3, primarily
due to domestic demand as new export orders eased, backlogs increased. Firms responded by lifting

employment, 1.5pts to 52.7. Against this backdrop manufacturers saw a renewed acceleration in cost
pressures, with the prices paid index jumping 6.8pts to 77.9. Firms cited higher steel and aluminium
prices, elevated energy costs, rising freight charges and ongoing semiconductor shortages, with every
major industry reporting higher input costs during the month.
The past week’s data will have done nothing to bridge the clear divide across the FOMC, as evident in

this week’s busy Fedspeak. Some participants argued for further rate hikes, citing strong inflation,
diminished supply capacity and policy that is not yet restrictive. Others counselled patience, pointing to
progress on inflation and that September’s hike buys time to assess upcoming data. The one thing
both agree on is that inflation will be the primary determinant of the timing and extent of further rate
hikes. Our view remains that one further hike is likely this cycle to bring policy closer to neutral.

In China, policymakers announced another round of targeted support measures aimed at stabilising
growth and supporting the struggling property sector. The PBOC lowered rates on its Pledged
Supplementary Lending facility by 25bps to 1.5% and expanded quotas for targeted lending programs.
Authorities also introduced subsidies for eligible first-home buyers. The subsidy applies to homes with
a floor area of 120m² or less and a value below CNY1.5mn, with support capped at 1ppt of mortgage

costs, up to CNY10,000 and for a maximum term of five years. The measures complement reforms
announced in August to improve developer financing conditions and support housing completion.
However, weak credit demand and confidence remains a significant constraint, suggesting policy
transmission is still limited.
The September PMI data pointed to a modest improvement in activity, although the details were less
convincing. Manufacturing returned to expansionary territory, rising 0.9pts to 50.1, supported by

stronger production and new orders. Much of the improvement, however, reflected a rebound in prices,
with the raw materials price index rising 7.6pts and producer prices increasing 6.2pts. Labour market
conditions remained weak, with the employment index falling a further 0.6pts and remaining below 50
for almost four years. Conditions in the non-manufacturing sector also improved, with the services PMI
lifting from 49.0 to 50.2, driven largely by stronger construction activity. Input and output prices moved

higher, selling prices returned back above 50, export orders improved to 48.5 and employment
increased 0.5pts, although at 45.9 it remains firmly in contractionary territory. Overall, the surveys
suggest activity is stabilising and policy support is gaining some traction, but weak labour demand,
subdued credit growth and soft domestic demand indicate the recovery remains fragile.
In Japan, the September Tankan survey delivered another constructive signal on the economy and

reinforced the case for further policy normalisation. Business conditions improved by 2pts for large
manufacturers and 5pts more broadly, while profitability indicators strengthened and capital
expenditure intentions remained elevated. Large manufacturers expect fixed investment to increase by
11.6% in FY2026, while large non-manufacturers expect investment growth of 11.2%, pointing to

### Page 4

Related articles
continued confidence in the medium-term outlook.
Capacity indicators improved further across both manufacturing and non-manufacturing sectors, with
measures of excess production capacity becoming less negative and indicating that demand remains
sufficiently strong to keep pressure on existing capacity. Labour shortages also intensified, with
employment conditions becoming more negative across most industries and firm sizes, pointing to a

progressively tighter labour market. Inflation expectations remained anchored around 2%, providing
further evidence that Japan is moving towards a more sustainable inflation environment. Overall, the
survey was consistent with an economy operating close to capacity and supports the view that a
December rate hike remains a realistic possibility.

## Document 212: Cliff Notes_ the importance of supply (2026-10-14)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ the importance of supply (2026-10-14).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: the importance of supply
By Elliot Clarke, Ryan Wells & Illiana Jain
09:47 September 25 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
Australia’s only data release this week was the August Labour Force Survey. Employment surprised to
the upside, rising 39.5k. But, once again, new labour demand was more than offset by supply as the
participation rate bounced 0.2ppts higher to 67.1%, just shy of January 2025’s 115-year high of 67.2%.

As a result, the unemployment rate increased from 4.5% to 4.6% and was close to rounding up to 4.7%
(4.65% to two decimal places).

### Page 2

There may be some residual seasonality in August’s print related to the new survey collection model.
However, the underlying trend is still consistent with a gradual easing in the labour market, seeing the
unemployment rate drift higher. We expect this to continue through to the first half of 2027.
The strength in labour force participation also speaks to the broader issue of Australia’s supply
capacity. As discussed by Chief Economist Luci Ellis, the RBA’s assumptions around participation,

productivity and the NAIRU collectively skew towards a weak assessment of supply capacity. For
participation in particular, the RBA’s near-term assumptions are downbeat compared to recent
demographic trends and also sit below Treasury’s longer-term projections as set out in the 2026 IGR.
As above, the August LFS emphasises that slack could emerge more quickly than the RBA’s forecasts
imply without an outright fall in employment.
Ahead of next week’s RBA meeting and inflation data, our latest state-level analysis provides

perspective on the persistence of underlying price pressures, particularly the importance of housing-
related components. For a broader assessment of the fiscal backdrop and the implications for the
states, our latest state fiscal update explores how the housing market correction and changes to NDIS
funding arrangements are pressuring state finances.
Before turning to this week’s events offshore, it is important to highlight that last Friday the Bank of

Japan raised its policy rate by 25bp to 1.25%. Policymakers maintained a constructive assessment of
the economy, pointing to resilient consumption, ongoing labour market tightness, and solid investment
spending. More importantly, the Bank expressed growing confidence that inflation is increasingly being
sustained by domestic wage and demand dynamics rather than imported cost shocks alone.
In our view, the emphasis placed on improving underlying inflation dynamics suggests policymakers

are becoming increasingly comfortable that inflation is able to be sustained by domestic demand and
wage growth. Normalisation is likely to remain gradual, but the Bank appears firmly on a path towards
further tightening, with rates likely to reach 1.5% by mid-2027.
This week, market participants have focused on discussions between the US and China on trade,
security and technology as President Xi visits Washington.
Preliminary discussions between US Treasury Secretary Bessent and Chinese Vice Premier He Lifeng

ahead of President Xi’s arrival in Washington were characterised by both sides as productive, and
Treasury Secretary Bessent subsequently announced that discussions in Washington would leave
trade negotiations for upcoming meetings between the two leaders in Shenzhen and Miami. To allow
room for this deferral, the current trade truce will be extended until January 2027. President Trump
subsequently outlined that discussions during President Xi’s Washington visit would instead focus on

security, technology and AI.
A more constructive tone was also seen between the US and Iran overnight, if press reports are
accurate. According to Bloomberg and Reuters, sources suggest the US and Iran are again exploring a
staged deal which would see Iran re-open the Strait of Hormuz and the US lift its blockade of Iranian
ports. Negotiations are occurring on the sidelines of the UN General Assembly, with Qatari officials

### Page 3

Related articles
mediating.
President Trump continues to show little interest in a deal before November’s mid-term elections, but
the global economy is under increasing pressure as the price of Brent oil hovers between USD100 and
USD110 while refining spreads add additional impetus to refined product prices. The mooted
consideration of a potential US diesel export ban this week makes clear that the US is not immune
from this pressure despite having significant domestic energy supply and refining capacity, relative to

their own demand.
The consequences of the current energy supply shock and other disruptions the US have experienced
over the past year remain front of mind for FOMC members. Those speaking this week such as NY and
Cleveland Fed Presidents Williams and Hammack highlighted the risk to inflation expectations from a
series of supply shocks when the economy is fully employed, and the likelihood of an additional hike(s)
being required to rein in current inflation momentum and risks. Williams noted that expectations of

another hike by year end are "reasonable" and that the FOMC have "a lot of work to do". Debt market’s
concern remains acute, having now almost priced in four hikes over the coming year and taken the US
10-year above 5.00%, currently 5.20%.

## Document 216: Consumer Checkpoint_ Strength in numbers September saw solid spending across many categories and a strong start to the holidays. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Consumer Checkpoint_ Strength in numbers September saw solid spending across many categories and a strong start to the holidays. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13026023     1

Economy 
Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers 
09 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Consumer spending remained very solid in September. Bank of America aggregated credit and debit card spending per 
household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY), or 5.1% excluding gasoline, both the third strongest growth rates over the past four 
years. Month-over-month (MoM) spending was more muted, especially without gas. 
• Spending shows considerable breadth: the vast majority of discretionary retail and services categories are showing growth. 
Moreover, this strength extends across income cohorts, with the ongoing recovery of lower- and middle-income households' 
spending growth. 
• With the winter months fast approaching, holiday spending is already increasing at a solid rate, though it's still early. In Bank of 
America deposit data through September, buy now, pay later (BNPL) usage is rising but not at a rate that is overly concerning. 
However, nearly one in every five households that use BNPL has a repayment of over $500.

Consumer Checkpoint is a regular publication from Bank of America Institute. It aims to provide a holistic and real-time estimate of US 
consumers’ spending and their financial well-being, leveraging the depth and breadth of Bank of America proprietary data. Such data is not 
intended to be reflective or indicative of, and should not be relied upon as, the results of operations, financial conditions or performance of 
Bank of America. 
A solid September for spending   
Consumer spending continued to look solid in September. Bank of America total aggregated credit and debit card spending per 
household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY), while spending excluding gasoline increased 5.1% YoY. Notably, both are the third 
strongest growth rates in the past four years. Seasonally-adjusted total card spending rose 0.2% month-over-month (MoM), 
while spending excluding gasoline was little changed. Overall, spending growth remains well above the 2025 average rate 
(Exhibit 1). 
Exhibit 1: Total card spending per household rose 6.2% YoY and 5.1% YoY excluding gasoline in September

Most spending categories are growing  
One striking thing about recent card spending, in our view, is that it is well balanced across categories. For example, both 
spending on retail (excluding gasoline) and services grew strongly YoY in September, at 3.5% and 5.4%, respectively. Meanwhile, 
retail spending is showing notably better momentum than in 2025 (Exhibit 2).  
Exhibit 2: Spending is rising across retail and services 
Retail (excluding gasoline) and services card spending per household (monthly, seasonally adjusted, index January 2025 = 100)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Well balanced across “nice-to-have” and “need-to-have” retail categories  
Is the momentum in retail spending reflective of “nice-to-have” discretionary or “need-to-have” necessity spending? In retail, 
this is a nuanced question, as many categories sit in a gray area between “necessity” and “discretionary”. General merchandise 
stores, for example, sell both essentials like groceries as well as nice-to-have electronics or even jewelry.  
In Exhibit 3, we account for this by grouping retail spending into “more discretionary” (furniture, electronics, building materials, 
online retailers, clothing retailers and miscellaneous stores), “partly discretionary” (auto, health and personal care retailers, 
general merchandise) and “mostly necessity” (gas and groceries). Even within these categories the edges will be blurred, but the 
overall impression is that retail spending growth is widely distributed, with spending rising across all fronts. One caveat: “mostly 
discretionary” spending has been making a smaller contribution since June. 
Exhibit 3: Retail spending is distributed across discretionary and necessity categories

balanced and broad-based (Exhibit 4). Non-store and online retailers and miscellaneous stores saw some of the fastest growth, 
while furniture and building materials were among the laggards, though other durable categories showed some strength.

For services, growth is driven mostly by discretionary spending  
In services spending, on the other hand, the boundaries between discretionary and necessity spending are often clearer. Travel 
spending (e.g., airlines, lodging) can be thought of as, by and large, discretionary. At the same time, utility bills, rent and 
insurance payments are necessities.  
And in services, discretionary spending remains the clear driver, accounting for over three-quarters of YoY spending growth in 
September (Exhibit 6). Leisure and housing services are major components of growth, though travel and restaurants also made 
positive contributions. However, as we noted last month strength in airline spending largely reflects price rises rather than 
transactions growth (read more in Consumer Checkpoint: Still sizzling).  
Exhibit 6: Services spending growth has been mostly driven by discretionary spending over the past year 
Percentage points contribution to growth by type of services spending (three-month moving average)

Source: Bank of America internal data

Lower-income households’ spending outpaced all but the top 5% 
The breadth in spending growth across categories is also evident across income cohorts. In September, lower-income 
households’ total card spending growth was 5.5% YoY, while the equivalent figures for middle- and higher-income households 
were 4.9% and 4.8% YoY, respectively. The top 5% of households by income continue to show the fastest spending growth, 
5.9% in September, though this has eased somewhat (Exhibit 7). 
Overall, the divergences across income cohorts are much smaller than they were in 2025 and the first three months of this year. 
Just focusing on spending excluding key necessities (gasoline, food and utilities) doesn’t meaningfully change the picture of 
convergence across income cohorts (Exhibit 8).  
Exhibit 7: The top 5% saw the strongest spending growth of any 
income cohort… 
Total card spending per household by income tercile and the top 5% 
(three-month moving average, YoY%, SA)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Exhibit 8: …though discretionary spending for the top 5% has eased 
some since June 
Discretionary card spending per household by income tercile and the top 
5% (three-month moving average, YoY%, SA)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The strength in lower- and middle-income households’ discretionary spending is occurring despite necessity spending shifting 
from a tailwind to a headwind over the past year amid rising gas prices (Exhibit 9). In our view, a key component of why these 
households have been able to combine rising necessity spending with strength in discretionary is the labor market and a 
recovery in their after-tax wage growth (Exhibit 10).  
In September, lower-income wage growth eased to 4.5% YoY from 4.7% YoY in August, while growth for middle- and higher-
income households rose to 3.8% and 3.7% YoY, respectively. These changes were modest, and lower-income households are 
continuing to see faster after-tax wage growth than other cohorts, a notable turnaround from the “K-shaped” pattern we 
observed over much of 2025 and 2026.  
Exhibit 9: Necessity spending has gone from a tailwind to a

Should we be concerned about the easing in household spending and after-tax wage growth for those with the top 5% of 
incomes? For now, probably not, as in our view this spending growth is likely being driven by wealth effects rather than wage 
gains. Absent a sharp correction in equity markets, there is little reason to expect them to retrench their spending. However, 
developments here are worth watching as this cohort comprises a significant proportion of overall consumer spending. They also 
tend to make outsized contributions to several discretionary spending categories (Exhibit 11).  
Exhibit 11: The top 5% of households by income make up a disproportionate amount of spending in highly discretionary categories 
Share of spending by the top 5% of households by income across select discretionary categories (three-month moving average to September 2026, %)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
With summer gone, thoughts turn to the holidays and BNPL… 
Now that summer is over, the next big gauge of consumer momentum is how people spend during the run-up to, and over, the 
holiday season. It is early days, but September spending on holiday items ran well ahead of 2025 (Exhibit 12). Given the backdrop 
of firmer after-tax wage growth and breadth across retail and services categories, this is perhaps not surprising. However, some 
of this spending strength could be due to rising prices as opposed to more purchases.

A concern often raised as we move into the holidays is whether consumers will lean on less traditional forms of credit such as 
buy now, pay later (BNPL) to finance their spending. Frequent BNPL users tend to have higher credit card utilization rates (read 
more in: Do consumers have wiggle room to absorb higher gas prices?).  
Looking at the share of households in Bank of America deposit data with a BNPL payment, we do tend to see a seasonal rise 
over the holidays (Exhibit 13), though in past years, these increases have quickly reversed. Further, while the underlying trend in 
BNPL usage is upwards, there are no signs of a sharp acceleration. We do, however, see some evidence that BNPL is being used 
to finance more expensive purchases, with the share of households making repayments above $500 rising, albeit relatively slowly 
(Exhibit 14).  
Exhibit 13: 8.3% of Bank of America households had a BNPL 
payment in September

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The tendency towards greater usage is occurring across income cohorts, though is somewhat more pronounced for lower-
income households (Exhibit 15). However, higher-income households have the largest share of BNPL payments above $500 
(Exhibit 16). Overall, in our view, the increase in the share of BNPL users heading into the season does not appear too 
concerning, though the rate of usage for some BNPL consumers does bear watching.

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Store category aggregation for “more discretionary”, “partly discretionary”, and “mostly necessity” is defined as follows: 
More discretionary: furniture, electronics, building materials, hobbies, miscellaneous stores, nonstore retail, clothing. 
Partly discretionary: auto, health and personal care, general merchandise. 
Mostly necessity: Gasoline, food and beverage.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts. 
The “more discretionary” retail category includes furniture, electronics, building materials, online marketplaces, clothing retailers and 
miscellaneous stores; while “partly discretionary” retail contains auto parts and services, health and personal care retailers, general merchandise 
and “mostly necessity”  retail contains gas and groceries. 
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

### Page 4

◆ The economy remains on the recovery track despite weakness in some
areas
Annualized real GDP growth in the second quarter was +1.1% QoQ, with growth
staying in positive territory for three straight quarters. Although personal
consumption exhibited weakness and capital investment declined, overall growth
was lifted by foreign demand. Exports, particularly of services, increased, and the
drop in imports of Middle Eastern crude oil also helped foreign demand turn positive.

◆ Housing starts are weak
Housing starts surged 18.6% YoY in June. They have continued to rise on a YoY
basis, but the jump was mainly due to a rebound from the slump in the same month
of the previous year. The recovery in the number of starts is actually moderate,
reflecting higher interest rates and soaring construction costs.
◆ Earthquake impact will need to be watched
Damage from the earthquake that occurred in Kumamoto Prefecture at the end of

July is weighing on economic activity in the affected areas. Although moves toward
recovery can be seen, with many companies having already resumed operations,
the impact could become prolonged in the following scenarios:
The first is if damage to capital stock such as factories and roads has been severe.
Some companies have sustained damage to production facilities, making an early
resumption of operations difficult in some cases.
The second is if a downturn in tourism demand occurs. According to some media

reports, there has been an uptick in cancellations of accommodation reservations in
Kumamoto Prefecture and neighboring prefectures since the earthquake. After the
2016 Kumamoto earthquake, the decline in travel demand from Japanese visitors
was short lived, but lower demand from foreign tourists persisted for a long period.
Annualized real GDP growth was +1.1% quarter over quarter (QoQ) in Q2 2026

### Page 5

Monthly Report of Prospects for Japan's Economy August 2026
The Japan Research Institute, Limited
Exports and inbound demand remain solid despite weakness in some areas
◆ Goods exports are on the verge of picking up
Goods exports are treading water. A sharp rise in memory chip prices is among
factors pushing up export prices, resulting in record high levels of exports in value
terms recently. On the other hand, the increase in volume terms is limited. One

possible reason is that Japan's export competitiveness has declined in fields such as
semiconductors, where global demand is growing.
Looking ahead, exports are expected to pick up. Capital investment by chipmakers
is expanding worldwide, and this should lift exports of semiconductor manufacturing
equipment, an area in which Japan has strength. However, continued caution is
needed regarding the effects of prolonged turmoil in the Middle East. A global

slowdown due to elevated crude oil prices could weigh on exports.
◆ Reluctance to visit among Chinese travelers is weighing on demand for
travel to Japan
Inbound demand is currently soft. The Chinese government's advisory urging its
citizens to refrain from traveling to Japan is having an adverse impact. The number
of Chinese visitors to Japan has fallen to about half the level prior to the advisory.
Meanwhile, the number of tourists from countries other than China remains firm.

Inbound demand is expected to stay robust going forward. Continued increases in
tourists from countries other than China will remain a supporting factor. The
expectation that the yen will remain weak will also act to push up inbound
consumption. However, there are two downside risks going forward:
The first is that the Chinese government keeps the advisory in place for a
prolonged period. When China banned group travel to South Korea in 2017, tourist

numbers stayed low for two to three years.
The second is the impact of higher crude oil prices. Major airlines have embarked
on phased increases in fuel surcharges, which could weigh on inbound demand
during the summer.

### Page 6

Personal consumption is gradually recovering thanks to a favorable income environment
◆ Higher disposable incomes are supporting personal consumption
Personal consumption continues to gradually recover. While consumption of
non-durable goods remains weak, services consumption is firm, particularly in
amusement-related services. In addition, consumption of durable goods has
increased significantly. Temporary factors such as the abolition of the environmental

performance tax on automobiles (since April 2026) and the tightening of
energy-efficiency standards for air conditioners (effective April 2027) have also
contributed to this rise.
A favorable income environment can also be pointed to as a background factor
behind the recovery in personal consumption. YoY real disposable income growth has
generally been positive since mid-2024, and the pace of increase has recently
accelerated. Employee remuneration is rising, partly as a result of agreements made

during the shunto (spring wage negotiations), and net asset income is also increasing
at a faster clip as companies hike dividends in line with stock price rises.
◆ Stock price developments could be a source of concern for personal
consumption
Personal consumption is expected to continue its gradual recovery going forward. In
addition to the favorable income environment and government policy measures to
curb price increases, a recovery in consumer confidence driven by easing tensions in

the Middle East will also provide a tailwind. Recently, the Consumer Confidence Index
has been showing signs of bottoming out.
However, a reverse wealth effect stemming from falling stock prices is a potential
cause for concern. The Nikkei Stock Average, which reached a new high in June, has
been struggling to push higher since the start of July. Estimates suggest the wealth
effect is concentrated among elderly households, but in recent years working-age

households have also increased their holdings of equity-related assets, and the
positive wealth effect for them may have supported consumption to some extent. For
this reason, if stock prices were to fall significantly, there is a danger of downward
pressure on consumption across a wide range of households.

### Page 7

セン
To
◆ The unemployment rate has settled in the mid-2% range
While the employment situation is generally favorable, weakness can be seen in
some areas. The unemployment rate has remained in the mid-2% range since 2022.
The number of employed persons in non-manufacturing, where business conditions
are favorable, is holding firm, while in manufacturing the number remains at a level
below that seen before the COVID-19 pandemic.
In the background to the drop in manufacturing employment is a slump in

production activity. Manufacturing output has been shrinking due to the effects of
U.S. tariff policy, soaring raw material prices, and a decline in the international
competitiveness of Japanese firms. As a result, pressure to reduce headcounts has
been building. Even in non-manufacturing, some industries closely linked to
manufacturing, such as transportation and wholesale/retail, are also exhibiting
weakness in labor demand.
◆ The employment situation could remain fragile in the near term

Looking ahead at the employment situation in the short term, a sense of
stagnation may persist. The contraction in output in the manufacturing sector will
continue to weigh on labor demand. Although production activity has been brisker
recently, particularly in semiconductor-related industries, the employment-creation
capacity of such industries is small. The employment-inducement coefficient per unit
of new demand tends to be lower than in other sectors, meaning that the industries

lack the strength to serve as drivers of employment.
However, in the medium to long term, the unemployment rate is expected to see a
gradual decline. Demand for labor is projected to stay solid, especially in
face-to-face services such as medical/nursing care and accommodation and
eating/drinking services, but the number of people seeking employment from the
non-labor force population has declined significantly due to the expansion in elderly
and female workforce participation. With limited room for expansion in the labor

supply, the job market is expected to tighten further.

The government is curbing inflation
◆ Policy factors are pushing down the inflation rate
In June, core inflation (all items, less fresh food) was 1.6%, staying under 2%
for the fifth consecutive month. Food price inflation has slowed, and a major

reason for this is a fallback in rice prices, which soared last year. Gasoline
subsidies are also continuing to curtail energy price increases.
Inflation looks set to hover in the 2% range in the second half of 2026. Although
energy subsidies and other government measures will soften inflationary pressure
to some extent, with imported inflationary pressure building, prices are expected
to be pushed up as higher costs are passed through to selling prices. From

FY2027 onward, the reduction in the consumption tax rate for food items will
serve to keep prices down, and inflation is expected to drop into the 1% range.
◆ The Bank of Japan (BOJ) will keep its policy rate unchanged
At its July monetary policy meeting, the BOJ decided to keep its policy rate
unchanged.
Long-term interest rates remain high. This reflects concerns about inflation
resulting from the increase in oil prices and expectations of an expansion in fiscal

expenditure.
The BOJ is expected to step up the pace of interest rate hikes while closely
monitoring the impact of higher rates on the real economy and financial/capital
markets. The next rate hike is expected to be in September.
Long-term interest rates look likely to rise gradually, reflecting heightened
anticipation of rate hikes by the BOJ and increased fiscal spending by the
government. If jitters about a deterioration in fiscal discipline resulting from such

measures as energy subsidies or the consumption tax cut take hold, there is also
a risk that long-term interest rates will rise.

◆ Surging crude oil prices have become a source of upward pressure on
prices
With crude oil prices having spiked against the backdrop of the worsening situation
in the Middle East, corporate input costs have risen sharply, and pressure for price
pass-through is intensifying. Looking at the Final Demand-Intermediate Demand
price indexes, the margin of price increases at the upstream stage far exceeds that at
the downstream stage, and price pass-through pressure is also substantial at the

downstream stage.
According to estimates based on past data, the impact of rising oil prices involves
a time lag. Specifically, the push-up effect on the consumer price inflation rate
increases for around 10 months and continues to exert influence for a long period
thereafter.
◆ Price pass-through willingness is also stronger than before
Notably, food product manufacturers are planning to raise prices for a huge
number of items during the summer and beyond. According to Teikoku Databank, the

number of items slated for price increases as of the end of July, when it conducted its
latest survey, was higher than in the May and June surveys.
If government subsidies for electricity/gas bills and gasoline prices are scaled back
or ended, both energy and food prices will increase concurrently, and the consumer
price inflation rate may rise again.
Attention also needs to be paid to the possibility that price pass-through driven by
elevated oil prices will become prolonged. In the 2020s, the rate of price increases in

transactions between companies has been higher than in the 2010s, and firms'
medium-term outlooks for selling prices have also shifted upward. Events that trigger
cost increases, such as heightened geopolitical tensions, have been occurring
repeatedly, and companies may be more willing to pass through higher costs to
prices than they were before.

### Page 10

The economy is expected to continue to recover, but downside risks are present
◆ The economy is projected to stay on the recovery track
The Japanese economy should continue its gradual recovery. WTI crude oil
futures prices are expected to fall moderately going forward.
In the corporate sector, government support for investment is providing a tailwind,
and demand for investment for labor saving and digital transformation remains
robust. In addition, rising global demand for AI-related goods should support exports.

Supply chain disruptions resulting from the deterioration in the Middle East situation
will begin to ease, and corporate production activity is expected to gradually recover.
In the household sector, inflationary pressure is expected to strengthen from the
summer onward due to higher crude oil prices. In response, the government will
probably ease the inflationary pressure through measures such as energy subsidies.
While the pace of increase in personal consumption is likely to slow somewhat,

consumption is expected to remain resilient amid continued high wage growth. From
FY2027 onward, the reduction in the consumption tax rate for food items should also
support personal consumption.
◆ The Middle East situation and rising interest rates constitute risks
Real GDP growth is projected to remain solid, registering +0.8% in FY2026 and
+0.9% in FY2027.
However, if the situation in the Middle East worsens again or becomes prolonged,
there is a risk that downward pressure on the economy will intensify, mainly through

three channels: 1) weaker exports, 2) raw material supply constraints, and 3) rising
prices. In addition, if market concerns about the fiscal stance of the Takaichi
government grow, the yen could weaken further, and long-term interest rates could
rise, potentially forcing the scaling-back or scrapping of expansionary policies such
as investment support measures, energy subsidies, and lower consumption tax.

## Document 294: new era of chip testing 20261008 ubs

Source PDF: all report pdf/new era of chip testing 20261008 ubs.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 31

### Page 1

ab 8 October 2026
Global Research
Asia Semiconductor Insight
AI put to the test: the new era of chip testing
Time to revisit semiconductor testing as content growth accelerates
Our core thesis is that AI-driven chip complexity is drastically increasing testing content
across wafer, package, and system levels, creating a multi-year growth opportunity that
the market has yet to fully price in. Testing companies in Taiwan have underperformed
TAIEX in the past three months (indexing at -9% vs TAIEX +7%), yet to recover from the

July market deleveraging. Investor hesitation currently stems from 1) fear of test content
growth expectations already priced-in, 2) lingering concerns over shorter test times, and
3) near-term capacity constraints among major suppliers of probe cards, load boards,
socket pins, testers, and handlers, caused by heightened testing demand. Ultimately,
these concerns overshadow the structural increase in test insertions required by AI
accelerators, chiplets, and advanced packaging. As advanced packaging scales, testing

intensity must rise in tandem, driving growth through higher content per device rather
than semiconductor unit growth alone.
Expanding testing opportunities with shift-left for CP and increasing FT content
Chip probing (CP) supply chain should be the key beneficiary of the industry's shift-left
testing trend, as more test insertions move to the wafer level before packaging, in
addition to longer CP time. We believe investors underestimate this trend, which should

accelerate with broader adoption of 2.5D/3D packaging, chiplets and larger AI
packages, driving demand for high pin count probe cards, probers, and thermal-control
equipment. We expect strong growth for Hermes Testing (HTSI)  as thermal-
management requirements rise with increasing chip power and complexity, with prober
thermal-control module shipments reaching 3,000/4,100 units in 2027E/28E, up from
1,200 in 2026E . We forecast Chunghwa Precision Test (CHPT) 's sales to exceed

consensus by 7%/15% in 2027E/28E, supported by structural AI/HPC testing demand
and rising value-added content. Importantly, shift-left testing does not diminish the role
of final test (FT) and mission-mode testing. Test content and test time should continue to
increase as validation becomes increasingly critical for complex, large-form-factor AI
accelerators. We expect larger package sizes and rising system-level testing
requirements to drive demand for WinWay's high-pin-count sockets, supporting

74%/71% YoY sales growth in 2027E/28E.
Future of testing: co-packaged optics (CPO) will be a major opportunity
CPO testing represents a significant new market opportunity, though we expect the
ecosystem to remain fragmented rather than consolidating around a single winner. As AI
scaling shifts the industry bottleneck from memory to interconnect, CPO adoption is
becoming increasingly important, creating new testing challenges that will require
collaboration across equipment, optics, and test solution providers. Leveraging

ficonTEC's optical alignment expertise and HTSI's probe, testing, and manufacturing
capabilities, we estimate the HTSI  will deliver 40 units of insertion 2 equipment and 70
units of insertion 3 testing equipment in 2027 , with further upside in 2028 as CPO
deployment expands from scale-out towards scale-up.
Initiate Hermes Testing, Chunghwa Precision Test, WinWay with Buy ratings
We initiate coverage on semiconductor testing solution provider Hermes Testing with

a Buy rating,  NT$8,880 price target based on 31x 2028E PE, for thermal control/CPO
with the most attractive risk/reward in our testing coverage; test interface PCB supplier
CHPT with a Buy rating, NT$5,000 price target based on 31x avg 2027-28E PE, for
CPU/TPU programs and test board content gain; and semiconductor test socket leader
WinWay with a Buy rating, NT$8,900 price target based on 30x avg 2027-28E PE,
for socket content growth and margin recovery. Among our testing coverage, we also

including information on the Quantitative Research Review published by UBS, begin on page 22.

HTSI, CHPT, WinWay, Hon Precision, Chroma ATE
LEAST FAVORED
N/A
Q: Is chip probing becoming increasingly important in the testing supply chain?
Yes.   As package costs rise, foundries must improve known-good-die (KGD) screening before
packaging, increasing demand for wafer-level testing. This should drive demand for probe cards,

probers and advanced thermal control solutions capable of managing higher power consumption,
wafer warpage and tighter test tolerances. We forecast HTSI/CHPT/WinWay' probe card business to
see a 31%/35%/33% sales CAGR for 2026-2031E, as they are positioned to grow strongly given
their smaller scale and robust growth drivers from shift left, CPU, AI accelerators. We forecast HTSI to
ship 1,200/3,000/4,100 thermal control modules in 2026E/27E/28E, as advanced wafer-level test for

AI accelerators will require thermal control solutions in order to prevent probe card needle burnt.
Q: Will FT and SLT content continue to grow in the coming years?
Yes. Despite concerns that final-test (FT) time could flatten or decline with Nvidia's Rubin GPUs, we
expect overall FT content to continue growing as advanced packaging complexity and chip sizes
increase. We also expect broader adoption of system-level testing (SLT)/mission-mode testing, as AI

accelerators require increasingly stringent validation to ensure optimal system and rack-level
performance. We forecast WinWay's socket business to see a 33% sales CAGR for 2026-2031E.
Q: Will CPO testing be a revenue generator in 2027-28 to supply chain players?
Yes. Testing remains a key bottleneck, creating a new equipment opportunity beginning in 2027E.
We expect initial CPO volumes in 2027, followed by broader adoption in 2028. Testing remains one

of the key bottlenecks to commercialization, driving demand for new equipment and test solutions.
We believe early leaders such as ficonTEC (partnership with HTSI) are well positioned to benefit from
the initial ramp, alongside suppliers including Chroma ATE and Hon Precision.
1) We believe upside from agentic AI CPU demand is not reflected in CHPT and WinWay shares. We
estimate CPU-related demand could contribute 20-30% of 2027E sales  for CHPT and WinWay,

respectively, with further upside in 2028E. 2) We believe market expectations for 2027 CPO testing
equipment shipments remain conservative, particularly for insertion 2 equipment. Based on our
industry analysis, we estimate HTSI will deliver 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems in 2027E, this
should be higher than the market's expectation of no insertion 2 and limited insertion 3 shipment
due to engineering bottleneck. We also see further upside in 2028E as CPO adoption expands.

Testing is becoming one of the largest incremental beneficiaries of AI-driven semiconductor
complexity. As more test insertions are required across wafer, package and system levels, testing
content per device should increase materially, expanding the industry's addressable market and
supporting multi-year growth across the supply chain.
TSMC and OSATs have been expanding their CoWoS capacities aggressively, from 40kwpm in 2024
to around 90 kwpm in 2025, and tracking to expand CoWoS capacity toward 160kwpm  in 2026E.

TSMC also ramped up expanding N3/N2 capacity, with multiple product launches from customers'
pipelines—Nvidia's Rubin, AMD's MI400 series/Venice CPU and other cloud ASICs—translating into
foreseeable, strong testing demand.

Source: UBS estimates
Shift-left igniting secular growth for chip probing
With the industry increasingly adopting shift-left testing, chip probing (CP) and its supply
chain should be key beneficiaries of a multi-year structural growth trend. This underpins

our above-consensus forecasts for Chunghwa Precision Test (CHPT), with 2027E/28E
sales 7%/15% above consensus driven by stronger AI/HPC demand, capacity expansion,
and rising value-added content. WinWay should also benefit through its partnership
with Technoprobe, with probe card revenue growth remaining robust at 94%/72% in
2026E/27E following 161%/260% growth in 2024/25.
Hermes Testing (HTSI) should be another major beneficiary as increasing chip power

and complexity drive demand for advanced thermal-management modules used
alongside high-end probing systems. With TEL's leading AI-oriented probers typically
paired with advanced thermal-control solutions, we expect HTSI's thermal-module
shipments to reach 3,000 units in 2027E from 1,200 in 2026E. Combined with testing,
installation and service revenue, this should drive HTSI sales growth of 127%/152% in
2026E/27E.
Accelerators still in focus with TPU as another significant growth driver

Nvidia remains a key growth driver for both CHPT and WinWay. We expect both
companies to gain share with Rubin, with WinWay's socket share improving from
roughly 50% historically to 60% with Rubin, while CHPT is positioned to supply around
70% of probe card and final test PCB content. Nvidia currently accounts for roughly
50% of WinWay's revenue and 30% of CHPT's.
Beyond Nvidia, we view Google's TPU roadmap as a meaningful incremental
opportunity. Following the successful ramp of TPU v8t with MediaTek as design partner,

CHPT is positioned to supply probe card PCBs, final test load boards, system-level test
boards and burn-in boards. WinWay will supply sockets for both TPU v8t and v9, with
the larger v9 package providing another growth leg. We estimate production of 4mn
TPU v8t units in 2027E and 2.3mn TPU v9 packages (9.2mn dies) alongside 2m v8t units
in 2028E.
CPU business as a supplemental catalyst for test interface in an agentic
world
With agentic AI proliferation, CPU demand is increasing significantly and this directly

correlates to increasing demand for testing consumables. WinWay and CHPT should
both benefit from the strong ramp up of Nvidia's ARM based Vera CPU and Google's
ARM based Axion CPU in 2026E and 2027E. WinWay will be supplying sockets and
probe cards for Vera and Axion, and CHPT will be the probe card and PCB supplier for
both Vera and Axion. WinWay will have the additional upside from AMD's x86 Venice
CPU, and CHPT could capture some share through AMD's equipment vendor

procurement. We estimate CHPT's CPU related sales to be low to mid-teens % of total
sales in 2026E and growing to 20-30% in 2027E  with CHPT's share expansions with
turnkey probe card solutions for Google's Axion. For WinWay, we believe CPU should
account for around 30% of sales in 2026E/27E.

### Page 4

Asia Semiconductor Insight 8 October 2026 ab 4
CPO testing fuelling future growth for semiconductor testing
ficonTEC's 25 years of precision optical-test expertise makes it an early leader in CPO test
equipment, particularly in optical alignment, a critical capability for high-volume
manufacturing. Through its partnership with ficonTEC, HTSI is well positioned to
benefit from the initial CPO ramp in 2027E-28E by supporting manufacturing scale-up.

We expect both insertion 2 and insertion 3 testing to be required over the next two
years. Despite the technical challenges of double-sided wafer probing, insertion 2
testing remains important given current optical-engine yields and the need to identify
known-good optical engines before fibre-array-unit (FAU) coupling. This becomes
increasingly critical as switch designs integrate larger numbers of optical engines.
We estimate HTSI will ship 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems in 2027E , with

further upside in 2028E as CPO adoption expands and next-generation platforms move
into volume production.
Testing TAM expanding with IC complexity: leaders likely to
outperform industry growth
Our central view is that testing content per AI accelerator is growing faster than
semiconductor unit volumes, creating a multi-year expansion in testing TAM. We believe
semiconductor testing is entering a period of structural growth as AI-driven chip
complexity increases testing intensity across wafer, package and system levels. The

industry's shift-left strategy, which moves more testing upstream in the semiconductor
manufacturing process, is resulting in longer and more frequent test insertions. As cloud
AI GPUs and ASICs become increasingly complex, additional testing is required to ensure
functionality, performance and yield, while reducing defective parts per million (DPPM).
More robust testing should improve deployment efficiency and lower the cost of
operating AI infrastructure.

Semiconductor testing generally comprises four stages: wafer/chip probing (CP), final
test (FT), system-level test (SLT), and burn-in. CP is conducted at the wafer-level before
packaging, while FT, SLT, and burn-in occur after packaging. The below Figure 3S e m i c o n d u c t o r  t e s t i n g  p r o c e s s :  f r o m  w a f e r  l e v e l  t o  p a c k a g e  l e v e l
illustrates the semiconductor manufacturing flow.
